Triage of 818 open and Pending Bug tickets of Tcl with no comment or check-in from me on 2026-10-16 (in Tcl's workflow Pending means "resolved, waiting for confirmation"). Reproduced on Linux (main 9.1 tip, 9.0 tip, 8.7b1, 8.6 tip, 8.5, 8.4 and old releases), on FreeBSD, NetBSD, OpenBSD and OpenIndiana VMs for platform tickets, and on a Windows 11 VM. Rows marked patch link to a prototype patch (287 in all, in tcl-triage/prototypes). Updated 2026-10-22: the emoji before each ticket id shows its progress.

      • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: The nested-mutex and condition-wait problems from this thread were handled in 893f8cc5db and fixed in 9.0.3 (cbb5b13ed1, d27472f8b2, fc66aa236c). Closing as a duplicate. The realtime-clock issue of timed condition waits deserves a ticket of its own if wanted.

      • Platform generic
      • Tested code reading only. The ticket only points at the tcl-core thread (no repro, and no network here). 8.6 has no TIP 509 recursive mutexes ("> 8.6").
      • Cause TIP 509 nested mutexes: Tcl_ConditionWait released only one level of a recursively held mutex (Unix and Win32), and tclEncoding.c depended on recursion. 893f8cc5db fixed the wait/unlock logic, tclEncoding.c no longer needs recursion, and mutex/condition tests went in (apn-mutex-tests, 2025-09-30). One point from the thread is still open: unix/tclUnixThrd.c Tcl_ConditionWait still computes the deadline from Tcl_GetDayTime() and calls pthread_cond_init(..., NULL), i.e. CLOCK_REALTIME, so timed waits follow wall-clock jumps. That is a separate RFE-sized change.
      • Prototype none (already fixed; the monotonic-clock part is a design change)
      • Suggested ticket comment The nested-mutex problems from this thread were fixed under 893f8cc5db (in 9.0.3 and 9.1). What is left is that Tcl_ConditionWait on Unix still uses the realtime clock (no CLOCK_MONOTONIC condattr). Close this as a duplicate of 893f8cc5db, or keep it open only for the monotonic-clock issue?

      • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: The 8.6 previous-day result is fixed in 8.6.16 by 595fad24d7. For 9.x it was decided not to accept leap seconds; leap-second acceptance was reverted and clock.n documents "leap seconds are not supported" since 9.0.1 (a5b130a5fa). Closing.
      Full verdict

      FIXED since 8.6.16 for the 8.6 day-off error (595fad24d7, 2024-11-15). The 9.0/main error for 23:59:60 is the documented behaviour (NOT-A-BUG: "leap seconds are not supported" was added to clock.n in a5b130a5fa, 2024-11-23).

      • Platform generic
      • Tested main, 9.0.5, 8.6.18, 8.6.16, 8.6.15. 8.6.15: free-form 2012-06-30 23:59:60 → 1341014399 (previous day; 24:00:00 also wrong). 8.6.16+: 1341100800, the same as the formatted scan. 9.0/main: "invalid time" with the default -validate 1. 24:00:00 → next day 00:00 on all of them.
      • Cause (8.6, fixed) ToSeconds -1 in the free-form scan; the remaining part is a design decision (POSIX time has no leap second).
      • Prototype none
      • Suggested ticket comment The 8.6 previous-day result is fixed since 8.6.16 (595fad24d7). In 9.x, rejecting 23:59:60 is now documented in clock.n (a5b130a5fa). Since the resolution is "Later" and the leap-second design question was dropped, this can be closed.

      Full verdict

      FIXED since 9.0.0 (zipfs path handling merged in 492fb812e9 / trunk 35dd9ec56b, 2023-10-15, ticket c3364db38a): only / is a zipfs separator, consistently

      • Platform Windows
      • Tested Linux main: only / works, consistently (all three backslash forms → 0; backslash is an ordinary character there)
      • Cause likely generic path normalization on Windows: //zipfs:\mt\... starts with two separators and is normalized as a UNC-like root (host "zipfs:"), converting all \ to /, while //zipfs:/mt\... keeps the backslashes in the tail that zipfs then looks up literally. Note TclIsZipfsPath() in generic/tclZipfs.c looks inverted: on _WIN32 it requires an exact "//zipfs:/" prefix, on other platforms it accepts \ for / (35dd9ec56b, 2023-10-15).
      • Prototype none (needs Windows to decide where to normalize)
      • Suggested ticket comment On Unix only / is a zipfs separator, consistently. On Windows the leading //zipfs:\ form is probably accepted only because the generic normalizer treats it like a UNC root; also TclIsZipfsPath() accepts \ on Unix but not on Windows, which looks reversed.
      Windows VM, run 4
      • Platform Windows
      • Verdict FIXED since 9.0.0 (zipfs path handling merged in 492fb812e9 / trunk 35dd9ec56b, 2023-10-15, ticket c3364db38a): only / is a zipfs separator, consistently
      • Tested VM win/29b19f5ef0.tcl (tests/zipfiles/test.zip mounted at //zipfs:/mt) on 9.1b1 and 9.0.5: //zipfs:/mt/testdir/test2 exists=1; //zipfs:/mt\testdir\test2, //zipfs:\mt\testdir\test2, //zipfs:\mt/testdir/test2 all exists=0 (the report had 1 for the last two). Same as Linux.
      • Cause TclIsZipfsPath() on _WIN32 now requires the exact //zipfs:/ prefix, so a backslash after the volume is no zipfs path at all. Cosmetic leftover: file normalize //zipfs:\mt\x gives C://zipfs:\mt\x (treated as a volume-relative native path).
      • Prototype none
      • Suggested ticket comment Since 9.0.0 (35dd9ec56b) zipfs paths on Windows accept only /, also right after //zipfs: (tested on Windows 11 with 9.0.5 and 9.1: all three backslash forms give &#91;file exists&#93; 0). Closing.

      🔧7e3f26c7482023Memory corruption false positive with TCL_MEM_DEBUGpatchFIXED since 9.0.0 (false positive: f505b17748, 2023-04-02) — but that fix turned the check into dead code. FREEDREFCOUNTFILLER is now 0xe8e8…e8, …

      Open ticket 7e3f26c748 · - New Builtin Commands · filed 2023-04-02

      🔧 Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

      Status now: Open

      • 🔧 2026-10-18 check-in 5495155636 on memdebug-freed-marker, not merged: Fix \[7e3f26c748\]: with TCL_MEM_DEBUG, the "previously disposed object" check could no longer fire, because freed memory was filled with 0x61 while \[f505b1774
      • 💬 2026-10-18 serhiy.storchaka: f505b17748 changed the expected refCount of freed objects to 0xE8..., but Tcl_DbCkfree still fills freed memory with 0x61, so the "decrementing refCount of previously disposed object" panic can no longer fire; a use-after-free decrement is caught only by the threaded check ("Tcl_Obj allocated in another thread", without file and line). The 0x61616161 checks in tclDisassemble.c and tclPreserve.c never matched on …
      Full verdict

      FIXED since 9.0.0 (false positive: f505b17748, 2023-04-02) — but that fix turned the check into dead code. FREEDREFCOUNTFILLER is now 0xe8e8…e8, while Tcl_DbCkfree still fills freed memory with GUARD_VALUE 0x61, so a freed Tcl_Obj has refCount 0x6161616161616161 and the "decrementing refCount of previously disposed object" panic (with file/line) can never fire. A use-after-free is caught only by the threaded-build check ("Tcl_Obj allocated in another thread", no file/line), and not at all in non-threaded builds. tclDisassemble.c and tclPreserve.c (size_t refCount) still compare against the 32-bit 0x61616161. 8.6: refCount is int and 0x61616161 references cannot fit in a 32-bit address space, so in practice the false positive is 64-bit 9.x only.

      • Platform generic
      • Tested main --enable-symbols=mem build (out of tree, work-a6/memdbg), with a small C program that decrements a freed object (work-a6/uaf.c): before → "allocated in another thread"; after → "file = uaf.c, line = 10 / decrementing refCount of previously disposed object". I did not run the 0x61616162-element lrepeat (~13 GB).
      • Cause generic/tclObj.c FREEDREFCOUNTFILLER does not match tclCkalloc.c GUARD_VALUE (the comment says it should).
      • Prototype prototypes/7e3f26c748.patch (26 changed lines, no test: only MEM_DEBUG builds are affected) — new TCL_FREED_REFCOUNT in tclInt.h = (size_t)-1 / 0xFF * 0x61 (0x61 repeated over the width of size_t), used by tclObj.c, tclDisassemble.c and tclPreserve.c. On 64-bit no real refCount gets that high; on 32-bit 0x61616161 cannot be reached either. obj/lrepeat/preserve/compile tests pass on the mem-debug build.
      • Suggested ticket comment f505b17748 fixed the false positive, but the new filler 0xe8… no longer matches what Tcl_DbCkfree writes (0x61), so this panic can never fire now. Proposed fix: compare against 0x61 repeated over the width of size_t (0x6161616161616161 on 64-bit) in tclObj.c, tclDisassemble.c and tclPreserve.c.

        • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Since Tcl 9.0 (TIP 602, cff0348cb6) a path starting with ~ is an ordinary relative name, so file join and glob treat ~abc.txt and ~* as files in the directory. 8.6 keeps its documented tilde expansion. Closing as fixed in 9.0.

        • Cause tilde expansion of ~user paths (8.x), removed in Tcl 9 by TIP 602.
        • Prototype none (8.6 behaviour is the documented tilde semantics; changing it there would be incompatible)

        FIXED since 8.6.9 (TIP 525 a4ea2b2387, 2018-10-29; repaired by 0ef0b36b31, 2018-11-08)

        • Platform generic
        • Tested main (own build c3bf6dd052), 9.0 tip, 8.6 tip, 8.6.8 — a child test file killed with SIGTERM under runAllTests

        • Tested main, 9.0 tip, 8.6 tip, 8.6.6, 8.6.8.         • Suggested ticket comment Fixed in Tcl 9.0 by TIP 402 (General Platform UNC Support, c1632c3763): on Unix a leading // is now kept by &#91;file join&#93; and &#91;file normalize&#93;. 8.6 keeps the old behaviour. On Windows, //server alone (without a share) is not a UNC root, so use &#91;file join //server/share ...&#93;. Proposing to close.

        Open ticket fbc88fb645 · 07. Variables · filed 2017-05-30

        ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

        Status now: Closed (Fixed)

        • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Fixed in Tcl 9.0 by TIP #278 (580ef6f047): relative qualified variable names no longer fall back to the global namespace, so $a::a and variable a::a agree. 8.6 keeps the old rules for compatibility.
        Full verdict

        FIXED since 9.0.0 (TIP 278, 580ef6f047, 2017-11-03 on trunk; 9.0.0 released 2024-09-25)

        • Platform generic
        • Tested main, 9.0 tip: in a proc in ::foo, $a::a and variable a::a now both refuse to fall back to ::a::a. 8.6 tip, 8.5.19, 8.4.20: $a::a falls back to ::a::a while variable a::a errors, as reported. (upvar a::a resolves in the global caller frame, so it is not a counter-example.)
        • Cause before TIP 278, relative qualified names fell back to the global namespace in normal variable lookup, but [variable] always resolves relative to the current namespace. TIP 278 removed the fallback, so the two now agree.
        • Prototype none (8.6 keeps the old documented resolution rules; changing them there would be incompatible)
        • Suggested ticket comment Since Tcl 9.0 (TIP 278, 580ef6f047), relative qualified names no longer fall back to the global namespace, so $a::a and variable a::a agree. 8.6 keeps the old behaviour on purpose. Closing as fixed in 9.0.

        Open ticket f4f44174e4 · 01. Notifier · filed 2016-12-05

        ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

        Status now: Closed (Fixed)

        • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Addressed in 9.0 by TIP 511 (1a6b389968): signal handlers should call Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal(), which is async-signal safe, instead of Tcl_AsyncMark(). Extensions such as TclX need to switch to it; nothing more is planned for 8.6.
        • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: What remains is in TclX: Tcl_AsyncMark() is still not async-signal safe (it locks a mutex), and TclX still calls it from its signal handler (generic/tclXsignal.c), so the hang in the script above can still happen. With Tcl 9.0 TclX should call Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal(asyncHandler, signalNum) instead, which takes no mutex. 8.6 has no such API; there TclX would need its own self-pipe or sigwait() thread to call Tcl_…
        Full verdict

        FIXED since 9.0 for extensions that use the new API (TIP 511 Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal, 1a6b389968, 2021-08-20, in 8.7/9.0); REPRODUCED (main, 9.0, 8.6) with plain Tcl_AsyncMark called from a signal handler, which is what the ticket (TclX) does — that usage stays unsafe by design and 8.6 has no alternative.

        • Platform unix
        • Tested work-a4/sig: small extension installing a SIGHUP handler + a child shell sending SIGHUP in a tight loop while the event loop runs pipe/timer events. Tcl_AsyncMark: deadlock (timeout) 5/5 on main and 5/5 on 8.6.18. Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal: 0 hangs in 40×3 s runs on main and 40×3 s on 9.0.5 (~4M signals per 8 s run). TclX itself not available here.
        • Cause generic/tclAsync.c:Tcl_AsyncMark takes asyncMutex (and Tcl_ThreadAlert takes the notifier mutex); a signal arriving in the thread that holds it (e.g. inside Tcl_AsyncInvoke) self-deadlocks. TIP 511 routes signals through the notifier thread with async-signal-safe calls. Branches bug-f4f44174e and aspect-async-pipe (2016/2017) were never merged and are superseded.
        • Prototype none (fixed by TIP 511 in 9.0; 8.6 would need new API)
        • Suggested ticket comment Fixed in 8.7/9.0 by TIP 511: signal handlers must call Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal() instead of Tcl_AsyncMark() (verified: no hang in 40 stress runs on 9.0/main, while Tcl_AsyncMark still deadlocks). TclX needs to switch to the new call; nothing more planned for 8.6. Branches bug-f4f44174e/aspect-async-pipe are superseded.

          ✅3608957fff2013Events posted by Tcl_QueueEvent linger in queueFIXED since 9.0 in practice (TIP 458 epoll/kqueue notifier waits in the calling thread, so the signal interrupts epoll_wait); REPRODUCED on 8.6 (th…

          Open ticket 3608957fff · 01. Notifier · filed 2013-03-24

          ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

          Status now: Closed (Out of Date)

          • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Tcl_QueueEvent() does not wake the notifier and must not be called from a signal handler. Since 9.0 the right way is Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal() (TIP 511, 1a6b389968) with the event queued from the async handler, and with the epoll/kqueue notifier the event is handled at once. Closing.
          Full verdict

          FIXED since 9.0 in practice (TIP 458 epoll/kqueue notifier waits in the calling thread, so the signal interrupts epoll_wait); REPRODUCED on 8.6 (threaded select notifier). NOT-A-BUG by contract: Tcl_QueueEvent does not alert the notifier, and calling it from a signal handler is not async-signal safe — TIP 511 Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal (8.7/9.0) is the supported way.

          • Platform unix
          • Tested sighup.zip attachment is truncated in the repository (1650 of ~231000 bytes); own extension work-a4/sig/qev.c (SIGHUP handler → Tcl_QueueEvent, [after 3000] "Boo!"), SIGHUP at 500 ms, 10 runs each: main and 9.0.5 handle the event at ~502 ms 10/10; 8.6.18 only at ~3000 ms together with the timer 10/10.
          • Cause 8.6 unix/tclUnixNotfy.c threaded build waits on a condition variable while the notifier thread does select(); the signal does not wake the waiting thread and Tcl_QueueEvent does not call Tcl_AlertNotifier.
          • Prototype none
          • Suggested ticket comment On 9.0 the event is handled at once (epoll/kqueue notifier, TIP 458); on 8.6 threaded it still waits for the next event. Tcl_QueueEvent/Tcl_Alloc are not async-signal safe anyway; signal handlers should use Tcl_AsyncMarkFromSignal() (TIP 511, 9.0) and queue the event from the async handler. Proposing to close.

          ✅3598300fff2012unix: tcl.h does not include sys/stat.hFIXED since 8.5.14 / 8.6.1 (590472b443 trunk / db771d0a61 core-8-5-branch, 2013-01-19)

          Open ticket 3598300fff · 52. Portability Support · filed 2012-12-24

          ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

          Status now: Closed (Fixed)

          • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Fixed in 8.5.14 and 8.6.1 by 992ef8a601 and 590472b443: tclPort.h now includes tclUnixPort.h (and so <sys/stat.h>) before tcl.h, also on macOS. In Tcl 9, tcl.h always uses struct stat on Unix.
          Full verdict

          FIXED since 8.5.14 / 8.6.1 (590472b443 trunk / db771d0a61 core-8-5-branch, 2013-01-19)

          • Platform unix (musl libc)
          • Tested code reading on main, 9.0 and 8.6 (no musl toolchain here). The 8.6 generic/tclPort.h includes tclUnixPort.h (which includes <sys/stat.h>) before tcl.h, and the Apple stat64 case is handled in tclUnixPort.h. On 9.0/main, tcl.h always uses typedef struct stat Tcl_StatBuf on Unix, so the musl #define stat64 stat problem cannot occur.
          • Cause tcl.h picked struct stat64 before <sys/stat.h> had been seen. The final fix (after the 8abba84224 revert because of OSX Leopard) put the platform port header before tcl.h. The leftover branch bug-3598300 (rebased 2018/2019, never merged) only drops HAVE_STRUCT_STAT64 on Apple.
          • Prototype none (fixed)
          • Suggested ticket comment Fixed in 8.5.14/8.6.1 by 590472b443: tclPort.h now includes tclUnixPort.h (and so <sys/stat.h>) before tcl.h. Tcl 9 always uses struct stat. Closing.
          ✅3588687fff2012Tcl_InitStubs() Broken As DesignedFIXED since 9.0.0 (mostly; dac224dbfc by jan.nijtmans, 2016-06-27, plus later magic handling in tclStubLib.c, e.g. 7704f91f2e, 2025-01-07). Par…

          Open ticket 3588687fff · 39. Package Manager · filed 2012-11-20

          ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

          Status now: Closed (Fixed)

          • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Fixed in 9.0.0: Tcl_InitStubs is now a macro that passes the extension header's TCL_STUB_MAGIC and major/minor version (dac224dbfc), so an extension built with Tcl 8 headers cannot pass the checks against a Tcl 9 stub table. It gets "this extension is compiled for Tcl 8.6" (7704f91f2e).
          Full verdict

          FIXED since 9.0.0 (mostly; dac224dbfc by jan.nijtmans, 2016-06-27, plus later magic handling in tclStubLib.c, e.g. 7704f91f2e, 2025-01-07). Part of dgp's branch bug-3588687 was merged to trunk for 8.6.0 (e738bbbfd9, 2012-11-29).

          • Platform generic (C API / build)
          • Tested read generic/tcl.h and generic/tclStubLib.c on main, 9.0, 8.6
          • Cause the ticket's problem was that the stub library's TCL_STUB_MAGIC, not the extension's header, was checked. In 9.x Tcl_InitStubs is a macro that passes the extension header's TCL_STUB_MAGIC and TCL_MAJOR/MINOR_VERSION (in exact) to the stub library. The library checks them against the interp's table and gives clear errors ("this extension is compiled for Tcl 8.6"). Tcl 8 headers (no version bits) are checked against the Tcl 8 magic, so 8-header + 9-libtclstub + 9 interp is refused. 8.6 keeps the 3-argument function, but all 8.x tables share one magic and layout, so the mismatch cannot arise there.
          • Prototype none (design change already done in 9.0)
          • Suggested ticket comment Since 9.0 Tcl_InitStubs() is a macro passing the header's TCL_STUB_MAGIC and major/minor version (dac224dbfc), so a header/stub-library/interp mismatch is detected at load time with a clear error. Proposing to close as fixed in 9.0.

          Open ticket 3522894fff · 53. Configuration and Build Tools · filed 2012-05-02

          ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

          Status now: Closed (Fixed)

          • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: The cause was the user's CPPFLAGS (-I/usr/local/include with older installed Tcl headers) coming before Tcl's own include directories. Since 8619c767c0 (8.6.12, and in 9.0) Tcl's own -I directories come first. Checked on Linux with a directory of decoy tcl.h/tclInt.h/tclPort.h passed in CPPFLAGS: 8.6.11 picks them up and fails, 8.6.12, core-8-6-branch and main build fine. Closing as fixed.
          Full verdict

          FIXED since 8.6.12 / 8.7a3 (8619c767c0 core-8-branch, 2019-12-24; 8.6: 70529bfafa, 2021-02-08)

          • Platform build
          • Tested unix/Makefile.in at the release tags: core-8-6-10 and core-8-6-11 still have ${CFLAGS} ... -I${GENERIC_DIR}, core-8-6-12 and later have -I"${BUILD_DIR}" -I${UNIX_DIR} -I${GENERIC_DIR} -I${TOMMATH_DIR} ${CFLAGS}. The main build log confirms that Tcl's own -I come before the user CFLAGS/CPPFLAGS.
          • Cause configure merges the user's CPPFLAGS (here -I/usr/local/include) into CFLAGS, and STUB_CC_SWITCHES put CFLAGS before Tcl's own -I dirs. An older installed tclTomMath.h/tclTomMathDecls.h therefore shadowed the source tree's copy and produced a reference to a missing TclBN_ symbol (pooryorick saw the same thing on Linux). Commit 8619c767c0 "Put our own included (-I) directories before directories coming in though CFLAGS" fixed it.
          • Prototype none (fixed)
          • Suggested ticket comment The cause was the user CPPFLAGS -I/usr/local/include (an older installed tclTomMath*.h) coming before Tcl's own include dirs. Since 8619c767c0 (8.6.12, 8.7/9.0) Tcl's -I directories come first. Closing as fixed.

The same check-in caused a new regression, filed as 4c5a98f5a0 (gets merges lines). Update 10-17: first triaged as NOT-REPRODUCED because only current versions were tested.

          • Platform generic
          • Tested (10-17): CRLF lines split at random points over a non-blocking pipe, -translation auto: 8.4.20, 8.5.0, 8.5.8, 8.5.9, 8.5.19, 8.6.0, 8.6.12 give 28 empty lines in 328; 8.6.13, 8.6.18, 9.0.5, main give none. -translation crlf, CR-only and LF-only input are clean everywhere.
          • Tested nonblocking pipe + fileevent with 10-char lines ended by \r\n, \n, \r, split at random points (300 lines) with -translation auto/lf/crlf/cr: no empty reads apart from the expected ones (\n\r input in auto mode gives an empty line per \r, which would be one explanation for the report if the device sent LF CR). While doing that: input "a\r" | "b" | "\nc\n" in separate reads gives {a bc} instead of {a b c} (blocking and nonblocking); with LF CR input some empty lines vanish. Old releases: 8.5.0–8.5.19 and 8.6.0–8.6.12 correct; 8.6.13, 8.6.14, 8.6.18, 9.0.5, main wrong.
          • Cause a3ed092aab (sebres, 2022-07-27, fix for b3977d199b, in 8.6.13) moved the reset of INPUT_SAW_CR in Tcl_GetsObj (generic/tclIO.c) so it happens only when an end of line is found. If the read after a trailing "\r" has no end of line, the flag stays set, and a later "\n" that starts a new buffer is dropped as if it were the LF of a CRLF pair.
          • Prototype prototypes/2953593fff.patch (against main; 39 changed lines, test io-29.36.3). Clears the flag as soon as any data follows the "\r", and sets it again in the restore: path when a skipped "\n" is pushed back (so the b3977d199b case, io-29.36.1/2, keeps working). Fixes all repros; io, chanio, ioCmd, ioTrans, encoding, zlib tests pass; io-29.36.3 fails without the fix. For 8.6 the bool needs to become int.
          • Suggested ticket comment The CRLF case does not give empty lines here. But a related regression from a3ed092aab (8.6.13): with -translation auto, "a\r", then "b", then "\nc\n" arriving in separate reads gives the lines "a" and "bc", because INPUT_SAW_CR is not cleared when the next read has no end of line. Proposed fix in Tcl_GetsObj, with test io-29.36.3.

          Open ticket 219237ffff · 27. Channel Types · filed 2000-10-26

          ✅ Since triage (updated 2026-10-22)

          Status now: Closed (Fixed)

          • 💬 2026-10-21 serhiy.storchaka: Since 8.6.0 (TIP #162, 3f8b48b6b6) both platforms resolve host names with getaddrinfo() and report the resolver's own message. On Windows an unknown host now gives "couldn't open socket: No such host is known." instead of "invalid argument" (checked with 8.6.18, 9.0.5 and 9.1).
          Full verdict

          FIXED since 8.6.0 (getaddrinfo-based name resolution in generic/tclIOSock.c)

          • Platform Windows
          • Tested main, 9.0.5, 8.6.18 on Linux with win/219237ffff.tcl (socket nonexistent-host-219237.invalid 80): couldn't open socket: Name or service not known.
          • Cause name resolution is now shared code, generic/tclIOSock.c:TclCreateSocketAddress (getaddrinfo + gai_strerror; on Windows gai_strerrorW), used by both unix/tclUnixSock.c and win/tclWinSock.c. So an unknown host should give "No such host is known." on Windows, not the old EINVAL from the WSAHOST_NOT_FOUND translation. The wording is the OS's own on each side, so it will never be the same text. Side note: errorCode stays NONE for resolver failures on all platforms.
          • Prototype none
          • Repro win/219237ffff.tcl (prints a VERDICT line)
          • Suggested ticket comment Since 8.6 both platforms resolve names with getaddrinfo() in generic/tclIOSock.c and report gai_strerror(), so the "invalid argument" message should be gone. To be confirmed on Windows, then this can be closed.
          Windows VM, run 3

          • Verdict FIXED since 8.6.0 (getaddrinfo-based name resolution in generic/tclIOSock.c)
